<debateSection name="#opening">

<heading>FRIDAY, 30th AUGUST, 1940.</heading>

<prayers>

<narrative>Mr. SPEAKER took the Chair at <recordedTime time="1940-08-30T10:35:00">10.35 a.m.</recordedTime></narrative>

</prayers>

<debateSection name="#questions">

<heading>QUESTIONS.</heading>

<oralStatements>

<heading>League of Nations.</heading>

<question by="#van_nierop" to="#prime_minister">

<num>I.</num>

<from>Dr. VAN NIEROP</from>

<p>asked the Prime Minister:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether the Government intends continuing its membership of the League of Nations;</li>

<li>(2) what sums have been paid during the past three financial years in respect of such membership;</li>

<li><span class="col_197-198" refersTo="page_0176"/>(3) when and where did the League hold its last meeting;</li>

<li>(4) whether he is in a position to inform the House of the likelihood of any further meeting of the League; if so, when and where is such meeting expected to take place;</li>

<li>(5) when was the last payment for membership made, and what was the amount paid;</li>

<li>(6) whether another payment is due and will have to be made; and</li>

<li>(7) whether the Government intends making such payment even if no meeting of the League should take place.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#prime_minister" to="#van_nierop">

<from>The PRIME MINISTER:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) Yes.</li>

<li>(2) 1937-38: &#x00A3;24,092.<br/>

1938-39: &#x00A3;24,136.<br/>

1939-40: &#x00A3;26,863.</li>

<li>(3) From 11th to 14th December, 1939, at Geneva.</li>

<li>(4) No.</li>

<li>(5) 6th May, 1940 &#x2014;&#x00A3;23,551 8s. 4d.</li>

<li>(6) Next payment due in September, 1941.</li>

<li>(7) The question will be one for future decision.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>War Expenditure.</heading>

<question by="#van_nierop" to="#minister_of_defence">

<num>II.</num>

<from>Dr. VAN NIEROP</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Defence:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) What was the cost of the present war to the Union up to 30th June, 1940; and</li>

<li>(2) what amounts thus expended were from (a) loan funds, and (b) revenue.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_defence" to="#van_nierop">

<from>The MINISTER OF DEFENCE:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) &#x00A3;10,469,467.</li>

<li>(2)

<ol>

<li>(a) &#x00A3;800,000.</li>

<li>(b) &#x00A3;9,669,647.</li></ol></li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Public Debt.</heading>

<question by="#van_nierop" to="#minister_of_finance">

<num>III.</num>

<from>Dr. VAN NIEROP</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Finance:</p>

<p>What was the amount of the public debt of the Union as at 30th June, 1940?</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_finance" to="#van_nierop">

<from>The MINISTER OF FINANCE:</from>

<p>The public debt as at 30th June, 1940, amounted to &#x00A3;290,028,000.</p>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Combating of Blow-Fly.</heading>

<question by="#van_nierop" to="#minister_of_agriculture_and_forestry">

<num>IV.</num>

<from>Dr. VAN NIEROP</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Agriculture and Forestry:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether he will make a statement regarding the negotiations conducted with a Mr. Smith concerning the means of combating the blow-fly;</li>

<li>(2) what steps, if any, the Government intends taking for combating this pest; and</li>

<li>(3) whether the Government will consider introducing legislation making the destruction of the blow-fly compulsory.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_lands" to="#van_nierop">

<from>The MINISTER OF LANDS:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) I am not in a position as yet to make a statement as I understand negotiations are still proceeding with Mr. Smith. A full report of the discussions up to the present appeared in a statement issued to the Press by the Wool Council on the 9th of July, 1940.</li>

<li>(2) Methods of eradication will always have to be applied by the individual farmer, but in order to assist him with improved methods, experimental work has been in progress for many years and is still being continued.</li>

<li>(3) As the hon. member knows, certain districts have been proclaimed compulsory areas for the destruction of carcases. Further legislation cannot be considered until such time as a more effective and practical method of dealing with the pest can be prescribed.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Civil Service: New Posts as Result of War.</heading>

<question by="#van_nierop" to="#minister_of_the_interior">

<num>V.</num>

<from>Dr. VAN NIEROP</from>

<p>asked the Minister of the Interior:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) How many new (a) branches, and (b) posts, which did not exist on 4th September, 1939, have been created in the Government service as a result of the declaration of war;</li>

<li>(2) (a) who are the heads of any new branches so created, and (b) what are (i) the designations of, and (ii) the salaries attaching to, their respective posts; and</li>

<li>(3) what is the total expenditure up to 31st July, 1940, caused by the creation of all such new posts and branches.</li>

</ol>

</question>

</oralStatements>

<p>[The reply to this question is standing over.]</p>

<heading>Members of Parliament: Military Service.</heading>

<num>XII.</num>

<from>Mr. JAN WILKENS</from>

<li>(3) what are (a) the rank, (b) the pay, and (c) other allowances, of each member.</li>

</ol>

<question by="#haywood" to="#minister_of_defence">

<num>XIII.</num>

<from>Mr. HAYWOOD</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Defence:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) What are the names of the members of Parliament who have (a) offered themselves for military service, (b) <span class="col_201-202" refersTo="page_0178"/>been accepted for military service, (c) taken the oath to serve beyond the borders of South Africa, (d) been sent beyond South Africa&#x2019;s borders, and (e) been promoted to military rank since 1st September, 1939;</li>

<li>(2) in respect of those promoted what was (a) their military experience, (b) their former rank, (c) their present rank, and (d) the pay and other allowances attaching to their respective ranks; and</li>

<li>(3) whether he has appealed to members of Parliament who have enlisted for military service not to serve outside South Africa&#x2019;s borders; if so, why.</li>

</ol>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Railways: Damaging of Train at Pretoria.</heading>

<question by="#haywood" to="#minister_of_railways_and_harbours">

<num>XIV.</num>

<from>Mr. HAYWOOD</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Railways and Harbours:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether soldiers smashed in the windows of a passenger train at the Pretoria station and raided the slotmachines at the station;</li>

<li>(2) how many window panes were smashed;</li>

<li>(3) what other damage was caused to the train;</li>

<li>(4) to what regiment did the soldiers belong;</li>

<li>(5) what is the total amount of the damage caused by the soldiers;</li>

<li>(6) whether the Railway Administration was compensated in respect of the damage; if so, by whom; and</li>

<li>(7) whether the soldiers were punished; if so, what was the punishment.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_railways_and_harbours" to="#haywood">

<from>The MINISTER OF RAILWAYS AND HARBOURS:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) Window panes were broken. No slotmachines were plundered.</li>

<li>(2) Four.</li>

<li>(3) No other damage was done.</li>

<li>(4) Various regiments.</li>

<li>(5) &#x00A3;46 16s. 6d.</li>

<li>(6) No.</li>

<li>(7) The occurrence was reported to the Department of Defence.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Railways: Charges of Nazi-Sympathy against Officials.</heading>

<question by="#haywood" to="#minister_of_railways_and_harbours">

<num>XV.</num>

<from>Mr. HAYWOOD</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Railways and Harbours:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) How many members of the Railway personnel have, in each of the following centres, appeared before senior officials on a charge of being Nazi sympathizers or of belonging to the Fifth Column, viz.: (a) Pretoria, (b) Johannesburg, (c) Pietermaritzburg, (d) Durban, (e) Bloemfontein, (f) East London, (g) Port Elizabeth and (h) Cape Town;</li>

<li>(2) how many of them, in each of these centres, were found guilty and what punishments were inflicted;</li>

<li>(3) whether, in those cases where members of the Railway personnel were falsely charged, steps have been taken against the persons who brought the false charges; if not, why not;</li>

<li>(4) how many members of the Railway personnel have taken the oath for military service beyond the borders of South Africa;</li>

<li>(5) whether cases of compulsion, intimidation or victimization by senior officials to induce railwaymen to take the oath have come to the notice of the Administration or of the Minister; if so, how many;</li>

<li>(6) whether such cases were properly investigated and any persons found guilty; if so, who were they; and</li>

<li>(7) whether he has taken steps to prevent members of the Railway personnel being compelled to take the oath; if so, what steps.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_railways_and_harbours" to="#haywood">

<from>The MINISTER OF RAILWAYS AND HARBOURS:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) None.</li>

<li>(2) and</li>

<li>(3) Fall away.</li>

<li>(4) I have no information in this connection.</li>

<li>(5) No.</li>

<li>(6) Falls away.</li>

<li>(7) Yes. The publication of a departmental Special Notice containing the following paragraph:</li>

</ol>

<p>&#x201C;There are naturally large numbers of men who desire to proceed on active service but in this connection it must be emphasised that recruitment for service outside South Africa is on a purely voluntary basis. This has been clearly indicated by Ministers on various occasions and the Minister of Defence has given the assurance in Parliament that there is to be no compulsion in recruiting men for active service outside South Africa. Some servants may, for one reason or another, not be disposed to volunteer for active service and their rights to refrain from volunteering must be respected. There must be no discrimination from a Railways and Harbours Service point of view between staff who are prepared to volunteer for military service and those who are not.&#x201D;</p>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading><span class="col_203-204" refersTo="page_0179"/>National Road Board: Provincial Allocations.</heading>

<question by="#haywood" to="#minister_of_the_interior">

<num>XVI.</num>

<from>Mr. HAYWOOD</from>

<p>asked the Minister of the Interior:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) What amount has been allocated by the National Roads Board to each of the Provinces for road construction;</li>

<li>(2) whether the National Roads Board instructed the provinces to economize to the extent of one-fourth of the amounts allocated, which saving was to be effected mainly on the staff;</li>

<li>(3) whether members of the National Roads Board encouraged road labourers to enlist for military service and to take the oath for service beyond South Africa&#x2019;s borders;</li>

<li>(4) whether a promise was made to road labourers that their work would be kept for them until they returned from military service; if so,</li>

<li>(5) in what manner will the National Roads Board keep open their positions in view of the fact that the Provincial Administrations were instructed to economize and mainly in respect of staff; and</li>

<li>(6) whether, in view of the instruction to the Provincial Administrations to economize on staff, they have the right, without the approval of the Civil Service Commission, to dismiss engineers and other officials engaged on road construction.</li>

</ol>

</question>

</oralStatements>

<p>[The reply to this question is standing over.]</p>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Police Force: Service Oath.</heading>

<question by="#haywood" to="#minister_of_justice">

<num>XVII.</num>

<from>Mr. HAYWOOD</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Justice:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) How many members of the Police Force (a) have taken their discharge from the service since 1st September, 1939, up to date, (b) took the oath to serve beyond the borders of South Africa and (c) refused to take the oath;</li>

<li>(2) whether any members of the Police Force were threatened because of their refusal to take the oath; and</li>

<li>(3) whether he is prepared to take disciplinary steps against senior police officers who threaten constables with victimization if they refuse to join up.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_justice" to="#haywood">

<from>The MINISTER OF JUSTICE:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1)

<ol>

<li>(a) By purchase 199, termination of engagement 2.</li>

<li>(b) 4331.</li>

<li>(c) 3348.</li></ol></li>

<li>(2) No.</li>

<li>(3) I have no information of any such case. If the hon. member cares to supply me with information on this point I shall cause the matter to be investigated with a view to suitable action if the facts warrant such a course.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

<question by="#grobler" to="#minister_of_justice">

<from>*Mr. GROBLER:</from>

<p>Arising out of the reply of the Minister, was his attention drawn to a sworn statement in Die Vaderland in which a certain person declared that he was actually threatened?</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_justice" to="#grobler">

<from>*The MINISTER OF JUSTICE:</from>

<p>If the hon. member will bring the case to my notice, I will go into it.</p>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<from>The Rev. C. W. M. DU TOIT</from>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Police Force: Service Oath.</heading>

<question by="#du_toit" to="#minister_of_justice">

<num>XXIII.</num>

<from>The Rev. C. W. M. DU TOIT</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Justice:</p>

<p>How many officers and men of the Police Force (a) have been dismissed, (b) bought or were compelled to take their discharge, and (c) have been transferred, for refusing to take the oath for service anywhere in Africa, and what was the name and rank in each case.</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_justice" to="#du_toit">

<from>The MINISTER OF JUSTICE:</from>

<ol>

<li>(a) Nil.</li>

<li>(b) Nil.</li>

<li>(c) One commissioned officer and fifteen other ranks, because they were out of sympathy with the Government&#x2019;s policy. Two additional commissioned officers were for the same reason transferred and in their own interest, and in the interest of the force, to areas where they could carry out their duties more efficiently. It is not desirable in their own interest to disclose their names.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Police Force: Dismissals.</heading>

<question by="#du_toit" to="#minister_of_justice">

<num>XXIV.</num>

<from>The Rev. C. W. M. DU TOIT</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Justice:</p>

<p>How many officers and men of the Police Force have been (a) discharged, (b) compelled to take their discharge and (c) transferred, since January, 1940, to date, and what was the name and rank in each case.</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_justice" to="#du_toit">

<from>The MINISTER OF JUSTICE:</from>

<table>

<tr>

<td><p>(a)</p></td>

<td><p>On ground of superannuation</p></td>

<td><p>67</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

<td><p>Medical unfitness</p></td>

<td><p>26</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

<td><p>Unsuitability</p></td>

<td><p>54</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

<td><p>By purchase</p></td>

<td><p>146</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

<td><p>Termination of engagement</p></td>

<td><p>1</p></td>

</tr>

</table>

<ol>

<li>(b) Nil.</li>

<li>(c) Owing to the calling up of the Police Brigade adjustments in personnel and establishment have been made at almost all the Police Stations in the Union. I do not feel justified in calling for the information asked for in view of the great amount of clerical labour involved in framing such a list.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Civil Service: Members on Active Service.</heading>

<question by="#naud&#x00E9;" to="#prime_minister">

<num>XXV.</num>

<from>The Rev. S. W. NAUD&#x00C9;</from>

<p>asked the Prime Minister:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) What was the number of male employees on 1st September, 1939, employed (a) permanently, (b) temporarily, <span class="col_207-208" refersTo="page_0181"/>in the following departments: (i) External Affairs, (ii) Interior, (iii) Labour, (iv) Social Welfare, (v) Agriculture and Forestry, (vi) Public Works, (vii) Justice, (viii) Lands, (ix) Public Health, and (x) Posts and Telegraphs;</li>

<li>(2) what were the respective numbers on 15th August, 1940;</li>

<li>(3) how many employees in the respective departments are on active service;</li>

<li>(4) in how many cases have the services of employees been dispensed with in order to effect economy in respect of their salaries;</li>

<li>(5) how many of those whose services were dispensed with were married;</li>

<li>(6) what are the total amounts saved in respect of each of the various departments as a result of the services of employees having been dispensed with; and</li>

<li>(7) what is the nationality or descent of such employees in respect of each of the departments mentioned.</li>

</ol>

</question>

</oralStatements>

<p>[The reply to this question is standing over.]</p>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Members of Parliament: Service Oath.</heading>

<question by="#naud&#x00E9;" to="#minister_of_defence">

<num>XXVI.</num>

<from>The Rev. S. W. NAUD&#x00C9;</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Defence:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Which members of Parliament have subscribed to the oath for service anywhere in Africa;</li>

<li>(2) (a) which of them are on active service, (b) where is each one serving, (c) at what rate of pay, and (d) in what rank; and</li>

<li>(3) which of them have been given office or clerical duties and what are their salaries.</li>

</ol>

</question>

</oralStatements>

<p>[The reply to this question is standing over.]</p>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Export of Oranges.</heading>

<question by="#naud&#x00E9;" to="#minister_of_lands">

<num>XXVII.</num>

<from>The Rev. S. W. NAUD&#x00C9;</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Agriculture and Forestry:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) How many cases of oranges have been exported since the last season, and to which countries;</li>

<li>(2) how many cases were exported by the African Realty Trust;</li>

<li>(3) what portion of the sum of &#x00A3;800,000 was granted to private farmers, and what portion to the African Realty Trust; and</li>

<li>(4) whether a certain person at Zebediela, although he did not export any oranges, received a grant; if so, why.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_lands" to="#naud&#x00E9;">

<from>The MINISTER OF LANDS:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) Up to August 27 orange exports to the United Kingdom amounted to 2,447,000 cases and 11,500 cases have been exported to India and the Far East.</li>

<li>(2) The Zebediela Co-operative Company has exported to date 722,091 cases, of which 339,400 belong to African Realty Trust.</li>

<li>(3) Private farmers and farmers&#x2019; cooperatives other than the Zebediela Co-operative Company were granted loans totalling &#x00A3;468,000. No loan was granted to the African Realty Trust, but the Zebediela Co-operative Company, of which African Realty Trust is a member, was granted a loan of &#x00A3;283,000.</li>

<li>(4) I have no knowledge of such a case.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Sugar.</heading>

<question by="#naud&#x00E9;" to="#minister_of_commerce_and_industries">

<num>XXVIII.</num>

<from>The Rev. S. W. NAUD&#x00C9;</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Commerce and Industries:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) How many tons of sugar were produced in the Union during 1939;</li>

<li>(2) how many tons were sold (a) locally and at what price, and (b) sold in England and at what price; and</li>

<li>(3) what was the amount of profit made by the sugar industry during 1939.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_commerce_and_industries" to="#naud&#x00E9;">

<from>The MINISTER OF COMMERCE AND INDUSTRIES:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) 579,639 tons (2,000 lb.).</li>

<li>(2)

<ol>

<li>(a) 293,909 tons (2,000 lb.) at the following prices:

<ol>

<li>(i) Refined sugar for direct consumption &#x00A3;21 15s. 0d. per ton Durban and Cape ports.</li>

<li>(ii) Refined sugar for use by manufacturers &#x00A3;13 to &#x00A3;14 per ton, f.o.r. Durban.</li>

<li>(iii) 2nd Grade sugar &#x00A3;13 10s. 0d. per ton, f.o.r. Durban.

All prices are net after deduction of the excise duty of &#x00A3;1 per ton.</li></ol></li>

<li>(b) 211,848 tons (2,000 lb.) at a net price of approximately &#x00A3;8 per ton on a raw sugar basis.</li></ol></li>

<li>(3) This information is not available. Many of the 600 planters included in the industry are unable to state with accuracy the profits earned by them.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading><span class="col_209-210" refersTo="page_0182"/>Cigarette Tobacco.</heading>

<question by="#naud&#x00E9;" to="#minister_of_commerce_and_industries">

<num>XXIX.</num>

<from>The Rev. S. W. NAUD&#x00C9;</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Commerce and Industries:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether he will ascertain and state&#x2014;

<ol>

<li>(a) the weight of tobacco required in the production of one box of 30 C to C cigarettes;</li>

<li>(b) the cost of production of one box of such cigarettes; and</li></ol></li>

<li>(2) what is the price received by the producer per pound for oven-cured tobacco.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_commerce_and_industries" to="#naud&#x00E9;">

<from>The MINISTER OF COMMERCE AND INDUSTRIES:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1)

<ol>

<li>(a) Between 1 and 1&#x00BD; ounces.</li>

<li>(b) Information of this nature can only be secured from the interests concerned under statutory authority and must be treated as confidential and not made public.</li></ol></li>

<li>(2) Last season&#x2019;s average price for all grades of oven-cured tobacco was 17.5 pence per pound.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Motor Cars and Farming Implements: Landed Cost and Retail Prices.</heading>

<question by="#naud&#x00E9;" to="#minister_of_commerce_and_industries">

<num>XXX.</num>

<from>The Rev. S. W. NAUD&#x00C9;</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Commerce and Industries:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) What is the landed cost of&#x2014;

<ol>

<li>(a) each of the following makes of motor car: Chrysler, Dodge, Plymouth, Studebaker, Oldsmobile, Ford and Chevrolet;</li>

<li>(b) each of the following makes of windmill: Samson, Star and Aermotor;</li>

<li>(c) a Columbia double share plough and of other plows of similar size; and</li>

<li>(d) each of the following makes of tractor: Farmall, Challenger (Massey Harris) and Fordson; and</li></ol></li>

<li>(2) what is the retail price of each of these articles.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_commerce_and_industries" to="#naud&#x00E9;">

<from>The MINISTER OF COMMERCE AND INDUSTRIES:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) (a) (b) (c) (d) Information of this nature can only be secured from the interests concerned under statutory authority and must be treated as confidential and not made public.</li>

<li>(2) As the retail prices for the articles in question vary according to sales for cash or credit, particular models and the places at which the sales take place, the hon. member will appreciate that it is not possible to give a retail price, for each of the articles, which would be applicable to the country as a whole.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Manufacture and Sale of Blankets.</heading>

<question by="#liebenberg" to="#minister_of_commerce_and_industries">

<num>XXXI.</num>

<from>Mr. LIEBENBERG</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Commerce and Industries:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) How many factories in the Union produce woollen blankets;</li>

<li>(2) what was the average number of blankets produced by these factories during each of the years 1938 and 1939;</li>

<li>(3) how many blankets have been purchased by the Department of Defence from these factories since the outbreak of the war; and</li>

<li>(4) what is the number of blankets for which orders have been placed and not yet executed.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<p>[The reply to this question is standing over.]</p>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Premier Mine Camp: Pneumonia.</heading>

<question by="#venter" to="#minister_of_defence">

<num>XXXII.</num>

<from>Mr. VENTER</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Defence:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) How many cases of pneumonia were there amongst the soldiers in camp at Premier Mine at the end of June, 1940;</li>

<li>(2) how many (a) nurses and (b) beds were in the hospital for the sick;</li>

<li>(3) whether the soldiers had to sleep on wet and cold ground without sufficient bedding and so contracted pneumonia;</li>

<li>(4) how many cases of pneumonia were there after 9th July;</li>

<li>(5) how many of the soldiers who took ill at Premier Mine between 15th June and 15th August, 1940, have died; and</li>

<li>(6) who ultimately supplied the hospital at Premier Mine with bedding, pillowcases, wash-clothes and other requirements.</li>

</ol>

</question>

</oralStatements>

<p>[The reply to this question is standing over.]</p>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Apprentices in Air Force.</heading>

<question by="#venter" to="#minister_of_defence">

<num>XXXIII.</num>

<from>Mr. VENTER</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Defence:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether apprentices and juniors in the Air Force are refused increase of pay and promotion on account of their unwillingness to take the oath for service anywhere in Africa, and in consequence are unable to obtain their wings; if so,</li>

<li>(2) whether he will give instructions that such increase of pay and promotion be granted immediately; and</li>

<li><span class="col_211-212" refersTo="page_0183"/>(3) whether, if increased pay and promotion have not been so refused, he will take such steps as will ensure that action of the kind indicated is not taken in future.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_defence" to="#venter">

<from>The MINISTER OF DEFENCE:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1)

<ol>

<li>(a) In the case of Permanent Force personnel, No.</li>

<li>(b) In the case of apprentices and juniors who have enlisted for service for the duration of the war only, and who have not taken the oath to serve anywhere in Africa increments and promotion are not considered, except in special cases.</li></ol></li>

<li>(2) No.</li>

<li>(3) No, it is unnecessary to take any such steps.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Bechuanaland Border Difficulties.</heading>

<question by="#du_plessis" to="#minister_of_external_affairs">

<num>XXXIV.</num>

<from>Mr. DU PLESSIS</from>

<p>asked the Minister of External Affairs:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether he has received any resolutions passed at a meeting of border farmers of the Union held at Bray on the Molopo River on 12th August last;</li>

<li>(2) whether he has, in accordance with his promise, been in touch with the British authorities since the last session of Parliament with a view to finding a solution for the difficulty which Union border farmers have for a considerable time been experiencing in consequence of prosecutions by the British Bechuanaland Protectorate authorities; if so, with what effect; if not, why not;</li>

<li>(3) whether he has submitted to the British authorities a proposal for placing at the disposal of the Union border farmers in the districts of Vryburg and Mafeking, subject to compensation, a strip of unoccupied Bechuanaland Protectorate territory along the Molopo River; if so, with what effect; and, if the proposal has been declined, what were the reasons given;</li>

<li>(4) whether he will comply with the request of the Union border farmers to have a proper conventional stockproof wire fence erected along the statutory boundary of the Union and Bechuanaland Protectorate at State expense, the cost to be borne by the respective Administrations in equal parts; if not, why not;</li>

<li>(5) whether he has discussed the criminal prosecution of Union border farmers by the Administration of the Bechuanaland Protectorate with the British High Commissioner; if not, why not; and</li>

<li>(6) whether it has come to his notice that such prosecutions are taking place at present.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_lands" to="#du_plessis">

<from>The MINISTER OF LANDS:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) Yes.</li>

<li>(2) Yes, for the time being the Protectorate Authorities agreed to deal more leniently with contraventions of the regulations relating to the straying of stock.</li>

<li>(3) Yes; the British Authorities could not see their way to agreeing to the suggestion that a strip of land along the border be leased to the Union Government, as this would have entailed a permanent loss of watering facilities to inhabitants of the Protectorate.</li>

<li>(4) This proposal is being investigated.</li>

<li>(5) See reply to (2).</li>

<li>(6) No.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Separate Railway Coaches for Europeans and Non-Europeans.</heading>

<question by="#van_nierop" to="#minister_of_railways_and_harbours">

<num>XXXV.</num>

<from>Dr. VAN NIEROP</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Railways and Harbours:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether provision is made on all branch railway lines for separate coaches for Europeans and non-Europeans;</li>

<li>(2) whether such provision is made on the railway line between Cape Town and Simonstown; if not, why not; and</li>

<li>(3) whether he will give instructions that steps be immediately taken to make provision enabling the European section of the public who desire not to travel in mixed coaches, to travel in coaches reserved for Europeans only.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_railways_and_harbours" to="#van_nierop">

<from>The MINISTER OF RAILWAYS AND HARBOURS:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) Yes, except in respect of the suburban service Cape Town&#x2014;Simonstown.</li>

<li>(2) and (3) It is not praticable to provide separate accommodation for European passengers; in this connection I desire to direct the honourable member&#x2019;s attention to the fact that separate travelling facilities for Europeans are not provided on the trolley buses and buses operating in the Cape Peninsula.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Soldiers and Sailors on Trains.</heading>

<question by="#van_nierop" to="#minister_of_defence">

<num>XXXVI.</num>

<from>Dr. VAN NIEROP</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Defence:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether soldiers and sailors are in the charge of an officer when travelling by train; if so,</li>

<li>(2) whether this also applies when they travel on the Port Elizabeth-Mossel Bay-Cape Town line;</li>

<li><span class="col_213-214" refersTo="page_0184"/>(3) whether dissatisfaction has been caused amongst a large section of the travelling public by the conduct of some of the soldiers and sailors when travelling by train, and in particular by their conduct towards women; and, if so,</li>

<li>(4) whether, in order to avoid any friction between the travelling public and soldiers and sailors, he will take steps to ensure (a) that units or groups of soldiers and/or sailors are not allowed to travel on trains without an officer in charge, (b) that instructions are issued to prevent any conduct that may give rise to dissatisfaction amongst the public and (c) that penalties are imposed for disobedience of such instructions.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_defence" to="#van_nierop">

<from>The MINISTER OF DEFENCE:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) Yes. Except when on leave when they travel as individuals.</li>

<li>(2) Yes.</li>

<li>(3) No case of unruly or objectionable behaviour to women on the part of soldiers travelling by train has been reported to me. If the hon. member knows of any case and will furnish details thereof, the matter will be investigated.</li>

<li>(4)

<ol>

<li>(a) No, the suggestion is not practicable in the case of details on leave travelling as individuals.</li>

<li>(b) Such instructions have been issued.</li>

<li>(c) Every case brought to the notice of the Defence Department will be investigated and disciplinary proceedings taken if justified.</li></ol></li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Native Trust: Purchase of Farms in Vryburg District.</heading>

<question by="#van_nierop" to="#minister_of_native_affairs">

<num>XXXVII.</num>

<from>Dr. VAN NIEROP</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Native Affairs:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) What stage has been reached in the negotiations in connection with the offer by Mr. P. E. van Rooyen of Vryburg of the farms Povall, Lotlapa and Vrygees in the district of Vryburg for purchase by the South African Native Trust;</li>

<li>(2) at what prices were the farms&#x2014;

<ol>

<li>(a) bought and</li>

<li>(b) offered; and</li></ol></li>

<li>(3) at what prices, under what conditions of payment and when, did Mr. Van Rooyen acquire the farms.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister" to="#van_nierop">

<from>The MINISTER WITHOUT PORTFOLIO:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) The farms have been bought by the South African Native Trust.</li>

<li>(2) The farms were offered at &#x00A3;14,091 but were bought by the Trust for &#x00A3;6,000.</li>

<li>(3) The hon. member is entitled to obtain this information from the Deeds Office.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Sultana Industry.</heading>

<question by="#conradie" to="#minister_of_agriculture_and_forestry">

<num>XXXVIII.</num>

<from>Mr. J. H. CONRADIE</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Agriculture and Forestry:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether the Department of Agriculture has instructed packers to buy up the whole or part of the 1940 sultana crop from farmers at fixed prices; if so, what prices were fixed;</li>

<li>(2) whether the Government has already sold the sultanas so bought; if so, to whom and at what price; and</li>

<li>(3) if the price for which the sultanas were sold is less than the price paid, who will pay the difference.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_lands" to="#conradie">

<from>The MINISTER OF LANDS:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) No, such an instruction was not issued. In order to protect the interest of producers, however, the Dried Fruit Board obtained a loan from the Government and this enabled the Board to arrange for the purchase of sultanas in cases where packers offered prices less than the f.o.r. equivalent of the prices at which sales to overesas countries could at that time be effected.</li>

</ol>

<p>The Board offered to purchase sultanas at the following prices per pound:</p>

<table>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

<td><p>Orange River.</p></td>

<td><p>Western Province.</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Supers</p></td>

<td><p>2&#x215D;d.</p></td>

<td><p>-</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>G.A.Q</p></td>

<td><p>2&#x215C;d.</p></td>

<td><p>2&#x00BC;d.</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>F.A.Q</p></td>

<td><p>2&#x215B;d.</p></td>

<td><p>2d.</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>A.Q</p></td>

<td><p>1&#x215E;d.</p></td>

<td><p>1&#x00BE;d.</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Undergrade</p></td>

<td><p>1d.</p></td>

<td><p>1d.</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Approximate average</p></td>

<td><p>2.28d.</p></td>

<td><p>2d.</p></td>

</tr>

</table>

<ol>

<li>(2) To Great Britain 1,400 tons have been sold at about &#x00A3;40 per ton c.i.f.; to Canada 532 tons at prices varying from &#x00A3;37 6s. 8d. to &#x00A3;40 per ton and to Eire 205 tons at about &#x00A3;37 6s. 8d. per ton and further sales are taking place.</li>

<li>(3) This aspect can only be considered at the end of the export season.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Klerksdorp: Reading of News Reports to Natives by Magistrate.</heading>

<question by="#jan_wilkens" to="#minister_of_justice">

<num>XXXIX.</num>

<from>Mr. JAN WILKENS</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Justice:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether he will make enquiries as to whether the magistrate at Klerksdorp interrupted sittings of the court for the purpose of reading out news reports to natives and commenting thereon; and</li>

<li><span class="col_215-216" refersTo="page_0185"/>(2) whether he will give instructions that news reports be communicated to the natives outside the court room.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_justice" to="#jan_wilkens">

<from>The MINISTER OF JUSTICE:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) The magistrate states that, in his capacity as Native Commissioner and acting on the instructions of the Native Affairs Department, he devotes a quarter of an hour each day to reading the war news to natives and warning them against communistic propaganda which is rife in that district. The news is read from the bench in Criminal Court daily at 10.45 a.m.</li>

<li>(2) I have no objection to this procedure, as the Court is first formally adjourned.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Klerksdorp: Statement by Magistrate.</heading>

<question by="#jan_wilkens" to="#minister_of_justice">

<num>XL.</num>

<from>Mr. JAN WILKENS</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Justice:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether he will ascertain if the magistrate at Klerksdorp, in his official capacity, used the following words from the bench, viz.: &#x201C;Iedereen wat die Regering se beleid teenstaan, behoort teen &#x2019;n muur geplaas te word en doodgeskiet te word&#x201D;; and, if these words were used,.</li>

<li>(2) whether he will have the magistrate warned not to express himself in this manner.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_justice" to="#jan_wilkens">

<from>The MINISTER OF JUSTICE:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) The magistrate reports that he made no such statement with reference to this country, but he did point out that there are other countries in which such conduct would lead to that result.</li>

<li>(2) Falls away.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Establishment of Meat Canning Factory.</heading>

<question by="#du_plessis" to="#minister_of_lands">

<num>XLII.</num>

<from>Mr. DU PLESSIS</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Agriculture and Forestry:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether he will consider favourably the recommendation made by the delegation of the Meat Control Board which recently visited Australia, for the establishment of a meat canning factory in the Union with state support; and</li>

<li>(2) whether he will seriously and favourably consider the representations which were made some time ago to the Department of Commerce and Industries in connection with Vryburg&#x2019;s claims to such an industry as being the most suitable place for its establishment.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_lands" to="#du_plessis">

<from>The MINISTER OF LANDS</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) The whole question regarding the establishment of a meat canning factory in the Union is at present receiving special attention by the Government.</li>

<li>(2) Should it be decided to establish such a factory, the most suitable centre therefor in the Union will be selected.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Investments in Foreign Life Assurance Companies.</heading>

<question by="#labuschagne" to="#minister_of_finance">

<num>XLIII.</num>

<from>Mr. LABUSCHAGNE</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Finance:</p>

<p>Whether he is in a position to inform and assure the House as to the safety of the investments of the public in life assurance companies which are not purely South African, should the currencies of the countries where the companies are domiciled, depreciate.</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_finance" to="#labuschagne">

<from>The MINISTER OF FINANCE:</from>

<p>I assume that this question has been addressed to me on the ground of my responsibility for the administration of the Insurance Act. I can assure the hon. member that the amounts to be deposited with the Treasury in terms of that Act have duly been deposited by all companies operating in the Union.</p>

</answer>

<question by="#labuschagne" to="#speaker">

<from>*Mr. LABUSCHAGNE:</from>

<p>I want to, tell the Minister &#x2026;.</p>

</question>

<answer by="#speaker" to="#labuschagne">

<from>*Mr. SPEAKER:</from>

<p>The hon. member can only put a question.</p>

</answer>

<question by="#labuschagne" to="#minister_of_finance">

<from>*Mr. LABUSCHAGNE:</from>

<p>The Minister possibly did not quite understand my question. The public would like to know whether people who have taken out policies with the insurance companies are fully covered.</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_finance" to="#labuschagne">

<from>*The MINISTER OF FINANCE:</from>

<p>So far as the law makes provision, they are covered.</p>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Handing in of Rifles: Prosecution.</heading>

<question by="#labuschagne" to="#minister_of_defence">

<num>XLIV.</num>

<from>Mr. LABUSCHAGNE</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Defence:</p>

<p>Whether he will seriously consider putting a stop to any further prosecution of persons who have conscientious objections against the giving up of their rifles.</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_defence" to="#labuschagne">

<from>The MINISTER OF DEFENCE:</from>

<p>No. Conscientious objections do not constitute any valid ground for disobedience to the law.</p>

</answer>

<question by="#marwick" to="#minister_of_defence">

<from>Mr. MARWICK:</from>

<p>Arising out of the Minister&#x2019;s reply could we have a record compiled of the people who have conscientious objections to surrendering their rifles?</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_defence" to="#marwick">

<from><span class="col_217-218" refersTo="page_0186"/>The MINISTER OF DEFENCE:</from>

<p>I have no information whether there is a record of conscientious objectors.</p>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Defence: Col. Van De Venter.</heading>

<question by="#du_toit" to="#minister_of_defence">

<num>XLV.</num>

<from>The Rev. C. W. M. DU TOIT</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Defence:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) What are Col. D. van de Venter&#x2019;s duties at present;</li>

<li>(2) what is his fixed pay per month; and</li>

<li>(3) whether he receives any special remuneration at present; if so, what amount per month.</li>

</ol>

</question>

</oralStatements>

<p>[The reply to this question is standing over.]</p>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Du Toit: Recruiting.</heading>

<question by="#du_toit" to="#minister_of_defence">

<num>XLVI.</num>

<from>The Rev. C. W. M. DU TOIT</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Defence:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) How many officers are specially engaged on recruiting work;</li>

<li>(2) who is the head of this division;</li>

<li>(3) what is (a) the minimum, (b) the maximum and (c) the total monthly pay for this work; and</li>

<li>(4) whether recruiting officers may stand drinks at hotels and in bars to prospective recruits at State expense; if so, (a) what control does his Department exercise on this expenditure and (b) what amount has already been spent in this manner.</li>

</oralStatements>

<p>[The reply to this question is standing over.]</p>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Postmaster General: Military Appointment.</heading>

<question by="#du_toit" to="#minister_of_posts_and_telegraphs">

<num>XLVII.</num>

<from>The Rev. C. W. M. DU TOIT</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Posts and Telegraphs:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether the Postmaster-General is on military service; if so,</li>

<li>(2) what is (a) his rank, (b) the nature of his work and (c) his military pay per month; and</li>

<li>(3) whether in addition he draws his full salary as Postmaster-General; if so, what is the amount per month.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_posts_and_telegraphs" to="#du_toit">

<from>The MINISTER OF POSTS AND TELEGRAPHS:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) Yes, in addition to his ordinary duties.</li>

<li>(2)

<ol>

<li>(a) Colonel.</li>

<li>(b) Director of Signals and Controller of Censorship.</li>

<li>(c) Nil.</li></ol></li>

<li>(3) He draws his ordinary salary as Postmaster-General.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Cape Town: Disturbances Between Union Citizens and Soldiers.</heading>

<p>The MINISTER OF DEFENCE replied to Question No. V by Dr. Van Nierop standing over from 27th August.</p>

<question by="#van_nierop" to="#minister_of_defence">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether the disturbances which occurred in Adderley Street, Cape Town, on 27th July, 1940, have been brought to his notice; if so,</li>

<li>(2) whether Union citizens were assaulted by soldiers of the Union Forces and by sailors; if so, what was the reason or alleged reason for the assault;</li>

<li>(3) whether the soldiers who took part in such assault included men from the Cape Town Highlanders Regiment;</li>

<li>(4) whether the Cape Town Highlanders Regiment formed the guard of honour at the opening of Parliament in January, 1940; and</li>

<li>(5) whether the military authorities have taken (a) steps against the soldiers referred to in (2), and (b) precautionary measures to prevent a repetition of such assaults; if so, what steps and precautionary measures; if not, why not.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_defence" to="#van_nierop">

<heading>Reply:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Yes.</li>

<li>(2) I know only of a particular case which formed the subject of a criminal prosecution in Court.</li>

<li>(3) A number of soldiers from the Cape Town Highlanders on the way to their homes after having been dismissed from parade, passed through Adderley Street and were called upon by the South African Police to assist in controlling the public amongst which a disturbance had arisen. Arising out of this incident a Private of the Cape Town Highlanders was convicted of assault in the Magistrate&#x2019;s Court, Cape Town.</li>

<li>(4) Yes.</li>

<li>(5)

<ol>

<li>(a) The Private referred to above has been discharged as a result of his conviction by the Civil Court.</li>

<li>(b) Yes, Military Police are on duty, in Cape Town and in the event of large numbers of visiting troops coming into the city military picquets are detailed for duty.</li></ol></li>

</ol>

</answer>

<question by="#van_nierop" to="#minister_of_defence">

<from>*Dr. VAN NIEROP:</from>

<p>Arising out of the Minister&#x2019;s reply, who asked the military to intervene?</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_defence" to="#van_nierop">

<from>*The MINISTER OF DEFENCE:</from>

<p>The police.</p>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading><span class="col_219-220" refersTo="page_0187"/>Aloe Plant.</heading>

<p>The MINISTER OF LANDS replied to Question No. VI by Dr. Van Nierop, standing over from 27th August:</p>

<question by="#van_nierop" to="#minister_of_lands">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<p>Whether his attention has been drawn to the beneficial uses of the aloe for medicinal purposes, as well as its economic value in certain areas; if so, whether he will introduce legislation for the protection of this plant.</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_lands" to="#van_nierop">

<heading>Reply:</heading>

<p>The Government is aware of the medicinal value of Aloe Ferox and there is already a considerable export trade in this commodity. The protection of the aloe plant by legislation is, however, a matter for the Provincial Administrations to deal with.</p>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Medical Services on Platteland.</heading>

<p>The MINISTER OF PUBLIC HEALTH replied to Question No. X by Dr. Van Nierop, standing over from 27th August:</p>

<question by="#van_nierop" to="#minister_of_public_health">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether his department is taking any steps to assist poor people who, in villages where there are no medical practitioners, cannot afford the high cost of calling in a doctor from a neighbouring town; if so, what steps; and</li>

<li>(2) whether his department will take the necessary steps to ensure that a medical practitioner pays weekly or bi-weekly visits to outlying villages such as Herbertsdale and similar places.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_public_health" to="#van_nierop">

<heading>Reply:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Yes. In terms of the provisions of Section 4 of the Public Health Amendment Act No. 36 of 1927, periodical visits are carried out by district surgeons in areas where the Department is satisfied that they are necessary. These visits cost approximately &#x00A3;11,000 per annum at present.</li>

<li>(2) No representations have so far been received for periodical visits to be made to Herbertsdale but the matter will now be considered by the Department.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Soil Erosion Schemes.</heading>

<p>The MINISTER OF LANDS replied to Question No. XV by Dr. Van Nierop, standing over from 27th August:</p>

<question by="#van_nierop" to="#minister_of_lands">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether work on soil erosion schemes is being carried on; if so, where;</li>

<li>(2) whether new erosion schemes will be undertaken; if so, what schemes;</li>

<li>(3) whether the construction of dams is to be proceeded with; and, if not,</li>

<li>(4) what steps, if any, have been taken by the Government in order to provide work for unemployed damworkers or labourers on erosion schemes.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_lands" to="#van_nierop">

<heading>Reply:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) and (4) Soil erosion works under Schemes A and B approved before the suspension announced on the 10th June, 1940, are still being constructed by farmers in different parts of the Union and the Department will honour its financial obligations upon satisfactory completion of the works. As regards Scheme C, for works under which the Department of Labour provides subsidised labour, my department only supplying plans and specifications, I understand that the Department of Labour has the whole question of subsidised labour for this scheme under review at present and pending a decision on this matter, the question of absorbing the labourers elsewhere does not arise.</li>

<li>(2) and (3) No new applications under Schemes A, B or C are being considered.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Police: Mr. C. A. Gagiano: Discharge.</heading>

<p>The MINISTER OF JUSTICE replied to Question No. XVI by Dr. Van Nierop, standing over from 27th August:</p>

<question by="#gagiano" to="#minister_of_justice">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether the policeman, C. A. Gagiano, who was attacked and seriously injured by a coloured mob in Cape Town on 27th March, 1939, is still in the Police Force: if not,</li>

<li>(2) when was he discharged and what compensation was paid out to him; and</li>

<li>(3) whether the Minister is willing to reconsider his discharge and the cause of his medical unfitness, in view of the facts of his case and the opinions expressed by two prominent medical specialists referred to in the House on 13th May, 1940.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_justice" to="#gagiano">

<heading>Reply:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) No.</li>

<li>(2) On 23rd May, 1940, a gratuity of &#x00A3;49 14s. 8d. plus an amount of &#x00A3;1 2s. 4d, being a refund of his contributions to the Union Widow&#x2019;s Pension Fund was paid to him.</li>

<li><span class="col_221-222" refersTo="page_0188"/>(3) In view of the medical reasons of discharge which did not arise from injuries that he received on the 27th March, 1939, I am not prepared to reconsider his discharge from the Force.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Midday Pause: Assault on Afrikaans-Speaking Citizens.</heading>

<p>The MINISTER OF JUSTICE replied to Question No. XVII by Dr. Van Nierop, standing over from 27th August:</p>

<question by="#van_nierop" to="#minister_of_justice">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether reports of repeated assaults on orderly Afrikaans-speaking citizens in Cape Town and other places during the midday pause of two minutes have been brought to his notice; if so,</li>

<li>(2) whether any criminal proceedings have been instituted against the guilty parties; if not, why not;</li>

<li>(3) whether the pause was instituted in accordance with any Government proclamation or municipal by-law or regulation; if so, under what authority; and</li>

<li>(4) whether he will immediately ban the pause in order to preserve peace.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_justice" to="#van_nierop">

<heading>Reply:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) and</li>

<li>(2) Six cases were dealt with; in two cases the accused were convicted; one case was withdrawn by the complainant; in one the Public Prosecutor declined to prosecute and two cases are still pending.</li>

<li>(3) The pause was instituted by the Local Authority.</li>

<li>(4) No.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Justices of the Peace: Conditions of Appointment.</heading>

<p>The MINISTER OF JUSTICE replied to Question No. XVIII by Dr. Van Nierop, standing over from 27th August.</p>

<question by="#van_nierop" to="#minister_of_justice">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether it is the policy of the Government to appoint persons as justices of the peace subject to the condition that they will perform their duties impartially and strictly in accordance with law, irrespective of political considerations, and that they have the confidence of the inhabitants of their district; if so,</li>

<li>(2) whether he will have inquiries made as to whether the conduct of the justice of the peace in the ward Armoed and surrounding area in the district of Oudtshoorn, complies with these conditions.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_justice" to="#van_nierop">

<heading>Reply:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Yes.</li>

<li>(2) Yes, if full particulars are supplied to the department.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Skins and Hides.</heading>

<p>The MINISTER OF LANDS replied to Question No. XXII by Mr. D. T. du P. Viljoen, standing over from 27th August.</p>

<question by="#viljoen" to="#minister_of_lands">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) What steps the Government intends taking for disposing of the hides and skins which farmers are unable to dispose of; and</li>

<li>(2) whether experts of the department are able to recommend a sure remedy for keeping moths from skins.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_lands" to="#viljoen">

<heading>Reply:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Every effort is being made to arrange for the sale of hides and skins overseas. In addition it is hoped to use increased quantities in the Union.</li>

<li>(2) A bulletin giving full particulars of the best methods of preservation will be released at an early date.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Handing in of Rifles.</heading>

<p>The MINISTER OF JUSTICE replied to Question No. XXIV by Mr. D. T. du P, Viljoen, standing over from 27th August.</p>

<question by="#viljoen" to="#minister_of_justice">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether Union citizens are being sentenced to hard labour in connection with the handing in of fire-arms; if so,</li>

<li>(2) whether the persons so convicted are required to have their heads shaven, wear prison clothes and to work in the company of criminals; if so,</li>

<li>(3) whether he will immediately take steps to have hard labour sentences annulled;</li>

<li>(4) why, for the same offence in connection with the handing in of rifles, the following different punishments were imposed, viz.: reprimands, fines, imprisonment, imprisonment with hard labour, in some cases for several weeks and in other cases for several months; and</li>

<li>(5) for what reasons are hundreds of rifles exempted from being handed in in some districts while in other districts under similar circumstances practically none are so exempted.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_justice" to="#viljoen">

<heading>Reply:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Yes.</li>

<li><span class="col_223-224" refersTo="page_0189"/>(2) The Prison Regulations provide that persons sentenced to certain periods of imprisonment, irrespective of the offence, must have their hair cut and beards trimmed sufficiently close to ensure cleanliness. Prison clothing is worn while the sentence is served, but as far as practicable, persons committed of failing to hand in fire-arms are put to work apart from other convicted prisoners.</li>

<li>(3) No.</li>

<li>(4) The sentences imposed are entirely within the discretion of the Court, which is naturally guided by the varying circumstances of each case. Appeals in these cases may be made to superior Courts.</li>

<li>(5) Government Notice No. 1069 of 1940 provides for three classes of exemptions. In class one (exceptional sentimental value) the decision is vested in the Chief Control Officer; in classes two (exceptional pecuniary value) and three (necessarily required for protection of the owner or his family) the decision is vested in the first place in magistrates who have a discretion, but whose decision to grant an exemption is subject to review by the Chief Control Officer. Each application for exemption is dealt with on its merits.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

<question by="#marwick" to="#minister_of_justice">

<from>Mr. MARWICK:</from>

<p>Arising out of the Minister&#x2019;s reply, is he able to tell the House of grounds on which the hon. member for Wolmaransstad applied for exemption?</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_justice" to="#marwick">

<from>The MINISTER OF JUSTICE:</from>

<p>He applied for exemption, and the magistrate gave him an exemption.</p>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Stellenbosch: Clash Between Students and Coloured Persons.</heading>

<p>The MINISTER OF JUSTICE replied to Question No. XXV by Mr. Haywood, standing over from 27th August.</p>

<question by="#haywood" to="#minister_of_justice">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether, in the recent clash between Europeans and coloured people at Stellenbosch, the police used their truncheons against the students;</li>

<li>(2) whether a police constable hit students on the head with a kierie;</li>

<li>(3) whether the police constable subsequently tore off his identification number and refused to disclose his name to the students;</li>

<li>(4) whether, on the following Monday morning, the students reported the incident to the captain in charge of the police; and</li>

<li>(5) whether he has had an investigation made; if so, with what results.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_justice" to="#haywood">

<heading>Reply:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Yes, to clear streets after due warning.</li>

<li>(2) No.</li>

<li>(3) No. One identification number was broken in the scuffle and removed by the policeman concerned.</li>

<li>(4) Yes. An affidavit was made by one Korglenberger, who later declined to proceed and destroyed the statement.</li>

<li>(5) Yes, fully investigated, but no charge was established.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Evacuation of Children from England.</heading>

<p>The MINISTER OF SOCIAL WELFARE replied to Question No. XXVI by Mr. Haywood, standing over from 27th August:</p>

<question by="#haywood" to="#minister_of_social_welfare">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) How many children have arrived from England in South Africa under the evacuation scheme;</li>

<li>(2) where have they been accommodated, and at whose expense; and</li>

<li>(3) what part of the expense in connection with the evacuation of children from England to South Africa will the Union Government be required to bear.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_social_welfare" to="#haywood">

<heading>Reply:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) None.</li>

<li>(2) On arrival the children will be placed in private homes with persons willing to take them at their own expense.</li>

<li>(3) The British Government bears the full cost of despatch and transshipment of the children to port of entry into the Union. The Union Government is meeting the administrative and secretarial costs of the scheme nationally and the cost of rail transport from port of entry to destination. The Union Government is standing as guarantor to the National Advisory Council in case there is a shortfall in private subscriptions. An amount of &#x00A3;5,000 has been set aside for this purpose.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Potchefstroom: Violence Against Bioscope Patrons.</heading>

<p>The MINISTER OF DEFENCE replied to Question No. XXVII by Mr. Haywood, standing over from 27th August:</p>

<question by="#haywood" to="#minister_of_defence">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether he will have an investigation made as to whether free bioscope tickets were issued to soldiers at Potchefstroom with a view to their assaulting persons who refused to stand when &#x201C;God Save the King&#x201D; was being played; and</li>

<li><span class="col_225-226" refersTo="page_0190"/>(2) whether he will have a charge brought if it should appear that the management of the bioscope was guilty of incitement to violence against peaceful patrons of the bioscope.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_defence" to="#haywood">

<heading>Reply:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) An investigation is not necessary, as from reports received it appears that when troops were first stationed at Potchefstroom in November, 1939, the playing of &#x201C;God Save the King&#x201D; had been discontinued. The troops protested against the failure to conclude bioscope performances with anthems and the manager thereupon undertook to play both &#x201C;God Save the King&#x201D; and &#x201C;Die Stem&#x201D; at the conclusion of performances. For the first few performances he offered complimentary tickets to troops but no arrangements involving projected assaults on any persons were made.</li>

<li>(2) Falls away as the management of the bioscope did not incite any person to any act of violence.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Cape Town: Damage by Australian Troops.</heading>

<p>The MINISTER OF DEFENCE replied to Question No. XXIX by the Rev. C. W. M. du Toit, standing over from 27th August:</p>

<question by="#du_toit" to="#minister_of_defence">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) What damage was done to (a) Railway or other Government property and (b) private property in Cape Town by the Australian troops who recently passed through;</li>

<li>(2) whether these troops caused any personal injury to people in Cape Town; if so, to whom;</li>

<li>(3) whether any of them remained in the Union; if so, how many;</li>

<li>(4) whether any of them lost their lives in the Union; if so, how many and in what way in each case;</li>

<li>(5) whether the Government paid any compensation for damage either directly or on behalf of the Australian Government; if so, what amount and in respect of what damage; and</li>

<li>(6) whether Australian troops are still allowed to disembark in the Union.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_defence" to="#du_toit">

<heading>Reply:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1)

<ol>

<li>(a) No damage to Railway property other than a few trifling losses, including glass and crockery broken or missing. No other damage to or loss of Government property has been reported.</li>

<li>(b) In a few instances slight damage to private property occurred, which was investigated by the Deputy Commissioner of Police. In all cases the persons concerned refused to submit claims for compensation.</li></ol></li>

<li>(2) No reports regarding personal injury to people in Cape Town have been received.</li>

<li>(3) Nineteen absentees were reported. These were subsequently despatched by the Naval Authorities to their destinations.</li>

<li>(4) Three lives were lost:

<ol>

<li>(a) one through accidental drowning caused by falling overboard.</li>

<li>(b) one through accidentally falling down the staircase of a building &#x2014; died from concussion.</li>

<li>(c) one through being knocked down by a motor car whilst crossing a street &#x2014; died from concussion.</li></ol></li>

<li>(5) No.</li>

<li>(6) Yes.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>League of Nations.</heading>

<p>The MINISTER OF EXTERNAL AFFAIRS replied to Question No. XXX by Rev. C. W. M. du Toit, standing over from 27th August:</p>

<question by="#du_toit" to="#minister_of_external_affairs">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) How many and which nations have already de jure or de facto left the League of Nations;</li>

<li>(2) why did the Secretary of the League of Nations. Mr. Avenol, resign;</li>

<li>(3) whether the Government intends continuing its membership of the League of Nations; if so, why; and</li>

<li>(4) what is the total average amount expended annually by the Union in respect of the League of Nations.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_external_affairs" to="#du_toit">

<heading>Reply:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Fifteen nations, namely:

Brazil<br/>

Japan<br/>

Germany<br/>

Paraguay<br/>

Guatemala<br/>

Nicaragua<br/>

Honduras<br/>

San Salvador<br/>

Italy<br/>

Chile<br/>

Venezuela<br/>

Hungary<br/>

Albania<br/>

Peru<br/>

Spain.</li>

<li>(2) Mr. Avenol advised member States of the League that since the Assembly, the Council and Committees could not meet at the present time; the duties which remained were principally the administration of the body of officials and management of financies of the League, which no longer justified the maintenance of the political high direction. The work of the technical sections could well be continued for the present in a form of organisation which <span class="col_227-228" refersTo="page_0191"/>would be better adapted to the needs of the hour than whilst effecting substantial economies. Therefore, for considerations of economy, he asked to be relieved of the task entrusted to him as from the end of August. He, however, offered his services in an advisory capacity or for purposes of information or explanation.</li>

<li>(3) The Government do not propose to make a change in the position at present.</li>

<li>(4) &#x00A3;26,711.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Railways: Transfer of Mr. Van Eeden.</heading>

<p>The MINISTER OF RAILWAYS AND HARBOURS replied to Question No. XXXI by the Rev. C. W. M. du Toit, standing over from 27th August.</p>

<question by="#du_toit" to="#minister_of_railways_and_harbours">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Why Mr. Van Eeden, a railway official stationed at Zeerust was suddenly transferred to the Witwatersrand;</li>

<li>(2) whether he served at Zeerust for approximately eleven years:</li>

<li>(3) whether his transfer was on promotion; and</li>

<li>(4) whether any complaints were recently received regarding his work.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_railways_and_harbours" to="#du_toit">

<heading>Reply:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) To meet the exigencies of the Service.</li>

<li>(2) No; 9 years and 7&#x00BD; months.</li>

<li>(3) No.</li>

<li>(4) No.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Railways: Discharge of Mr. J. G. Uys.</heading>

<p>The MINISTER OF RAILWAYS AND HARBOURS replied to Question No. XXXII by the Rev. C. W. M. du Toit, standing over from the 27th August.</p>

<question by="#du_toit" to="#minister_of_railways_and_harbours">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Why Mr. J. G. Uys, who was in charge of the power station at De Aar was discharged;</li>

<li>(2) whether he was ordered to do work below his grade; if so, why;</li>

<li>(3) by whom was he replaced in the power station;</li>

<li>(4) whether the person who replaced him was, at the time when Uys was first ordered to leave the power station, competent and qualified to do the work attaching to the post;</li>

<li>(5) whether there was any trouble in the power station after he had been replaced; if so, of what nature;</li>

<li>(6) whether the Minister will enquire into the facts with a view to removing any injustice that may have been done to him; and</li>

<li>(7) whether complaints had been received about the discharge of his duties in the power station; if so, what complaints.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_railways_and_harbours" to="#du_toit">

<heading>Reply:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) to (7) This servant has been charged under the departmental disciplinary regulations, and as the matter is still sub judice, the honourable member will readily appreciate that it is not possible to furnish further particulars at this stage.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Railways: Victimization of Bechuanaland Permanent Way Inspector.</heading>

<p>The MINISTER OF RAILWAYS AND HARBOURS replied to Question No. XXXIII by the Rev. C. W. M. du Toit, standing over from the 27th August.</p>

<question by="#du_toit" to="#minister_of_railways_and_harbours">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Why was the permanent way inspector in the Bechuanaland Protectorate transferred to De Aar;</li>

<li>(2) whether the system engineer made use of the following words to the officer concerned, viz.: &#x2018;I don&#x2019;t want any Nationalists about here; you are a Nationalist and a Nazi; I&#x2019;ll have you transferred to De Aar until I can find a place good or bad enough for you,&#x201D; or words to that effect; and</li>

<li>(3) if such words were used, how does the Minister reconcile the incident with statements made by him at Mossel Bay and elsewhere to the effect that there should be no victimization in the railway service because of political convictions of an officer or employee, as well as with Circular No. 2620 of the General Manager.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_railways_and_harbours" to="#du_toit">

<heading>Reply:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Permanent Way Inspector Van der Schyff, Mahalapye, made several applications for transfer to the Union, and he was utilized for relief duty at De Aar pending arrangements being made for his transfer to the Union. His transfer to Bethlehem has now been arranged.</li>

<li>(2) No.</li>

<li>(3) Falls away.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>De Aar: Transfer of Railway Ganger.</heading>

<p>The MINISTER OF RAILWAYS AND HARBOURS replied to Question No. XXXIV by the Rev. C. W. M. du Toit, standing over from 27th August.</p>

<question by="#du_toit" to="#minister_of_railways_and_harbours">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Why a certain railway ganger, who had been stationed at De Aar for approximately ten years, was suddenly transferred from that station to Natal;</li>

<li><span class="col_229-230" refersTo="page_0192"/>(2) whether the transfer of this employee took place five months prior to the date when he would have been retired on pension, two months of this period being due to him as leave; and</li>

<li>(3) whether there had been any complaints against the work of this ganger at De Aar.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_railways_and_harbours" to="#du_toit">

<heading>Reply:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) To meet the exigencies of the Service.</li>

<li>(2) Yes. He has approximately two months&#x2019; leave standing to his credit but desires to work the full five months, and thereafter receive payment in respect of leave due.</li>

<li>(3) Yes.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Kimberley: Medical Examination of Applicants for Technical Training.</heading>

<p>The MINISTER OF THE INTERIOR replied to Question No. XXXVII by the Rev. C. W. M. du Toit, standing over from 27th August.</p>

<question by="#du_toit" to="#minister_of_the_interior">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether a certain doctor who was acting as district surgeon at Kimberley on at least four occasions refused to examine applicants medically for technical military training unless they submitted forms in English; if so, what were the reasons for such refusal; and</li>

<li>(2) whether this doctor is bilingual.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_the_interior" to="#du_toit">

<heading>Reply:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) A Dr. Patton was employed by the Municipality as Assistant Medical Officer of Health for a short period in February, 1940, during which time he also acted as locum tenens for the assistant district surgeon. Complaints were received that he refused to examine two applicants under the technical training scheme because they submitted forms in Afrikaans. Efforts to obtain an explanation from Dr. Patton have been unsuccessful as he has left Kimberley. It is understood that he is no longer in the Union.</li>

<li>(2) It is not known whether the doctor is bilingual.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Southern Rhodesia: Calling up of Union Nationals.</heading>

<p>The PRIME MINISTER replied to Question No. XL by Mr. J. G. Strydom, standing over from 27th August:</p>

<question by="#strydom" to="#prime_minister">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether, in view of a decision by the Military Court in Southern Rhodesia that Union nationals residing there are liable to be called up for military service and of the fact that this Court interpreted Union nationality as only a form of British nationality, he has made representations to the Government of Southern Rhodesia with a view to preventing Union nationals residing in Rhodesia being called up; and</li>

<li>(2) whether, in view of this interpretation, the Government will take steps to amend the Union laws relating to nationality in such a manner as completely to abolish double nationality and to prevent any Union national being considered a British subject.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#prime_minister" to="#strydom">

<heading>Reply:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) The Union Government are taking steps in order to arrive at a satisfactory arrangement with the Government of Southern Rhodesia to obtain the exemption of Union nationals in that territory from liability for military service under Southern Rhodesian law.</li>

<li>(2) No. The question of double nationality is not involved in this matter.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Price of Maize in Cape Town.</heading>

<p>The MINISTER OF LANDS replied to Question No. XLIII by Rev. S. W. Naud&#x00E9;, standing over from 27th August:</p>

<question by="#naud&#x00E9;" to="#minister_of_lands">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<p>What is the price per bag of (a) white maize, and (b) mealie meal in Cape Town.</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_lands" to="#naud&#x00E9;">

<heading>Reply:</heading>

<p>The price in Cape Town of (a) white maize is 12s. 3d. per bag of 200 lbs. and (b) mealie meal 12s. 3d. per bag of 180 lbs.</p>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Refugee Children.</heading>

<p>The MINISTER OF SOCIAL WELFARE replied to Question No. L by Mr. Louw, standing over from the 27th August:</p>

<question by="#louw" to="#minister_of_social_welfare">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) How many evacuated and refugee children have arrived in the Union to date:</li>

<li>(2) What is the total number of children the Union Government has agreed to receive in South Africa; and</li>

<li>(3) whether the Union Government obtained written undertakings from the British and other governments concerned that such children will be taken back on the termination of the war.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_social_welfare" to="#louw">

<heading>Reply:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) None under the evacuation scheme.</li>

<li>(2) No specific number has been stated but the Union Government is prepared to <span class="col_231-232" refersTo="page_0193"/>accept children up to the maximum absorptive capacity of the country.</li>

<li>(3) All the children are being accorded temporary permits to enter into the Union. The Union Government has indicated its willingness to consider the children for permanent residence, but the British Government has replied to the effect that it wants its children back on the termination of the war.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Defence: Calling up of J. R. Lochner in Southern Rhodesia.</heading>

<p>The PRIME MINISTER replied to Question No. LII by Mr. Fagan, standing over from the 27th August:</p>

<question by="#fagan" to="#prime_minister">

<heading>Question:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether his attention or that of his department has been drawn to a Press report of 21st August, 1940, in which it is stated that a Mr. J. R. Lochner, a Union national, has been called up for compulsory military service in Southern Rhodesia, that his application for a permit to return to the Union was refused and that his appeal to the Military Court for exemption from military duty to enable him to return to the Union was also refused and that he was ordered to report for service;</li>

<li>(2) whether the Dutch Reformed Church or any other denominations have made representations to the Government concerning the possibility of a large number of missionaries who are Union nationals stationed in Rhodesia and Nyasaland being called up;</li>

<li>(3) whether, in view of the case referred to and the position of the missionaries and other Union nationals in the British colonies in Africa, the Government has made representations to the Governments concerned regarding the calling up of Union nationals; if so,</li>

<li>(4) what were the nature and the effect of the representations made; and, if not,</li>

<li>(5) whether the Government has taken or intends taking other steps in connection with the matter; if so, what steps.</li>

</ol>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_social_welfare" to="#louw">

<heading>Reply:</heading>

<ol>

<li>(1) Yes. I have seen the Press report.</li>

<li>(2) No.</li>

<li>(3) and (4) The Union Government are taking steps in order to arrive at a satisfactory arrangement with the Government of Southern Rhodesia to obtain the exemption of Union nationals in that territory from liability for military service under Southern Rhodesian law.<br/>

The Union Government have no knowledge of Union nationals being called up in any other territory.</li>

<li>(5) The intention of the Union Government is to come to the same arrangement with the Government of Southern Rhodesia as exists between the Union Government and the British Government.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

<question by="#hertzog" to="#prime_minister">

<from>*Gen. HERTZOG:</from>
